/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kaidoku-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/2026-10-28-r49-squashfs-v1v2-core.md:55:- **The first reader, sasquatch, is a patch set over the same upstream codebase.** The sasquatch README (S111, row in `corpus/SOURCES.md`) describes it as a patch set over the standard `unsquashfs`, and its build script downloads squashfs-tools v4.3, patches it and builds (`corpus/knowledge/2026-10-26-unblob-sasquatch-dependency.md`). The onekey-sec package the lane installed reports `unsquashfs version 4.5.1`, and the release body of `sasquatch-v4.5.1-1` says it is rebased on squashfs-tools 4.5.1 (`corpus/knowledge/2026-10-27-sasquatch-release-matrix.md`). The two readers therefore descend from one squashfs-tools codebase through different bases and patch sets. This is the difference the entry relies on, and it is not an independent-implementation claim: the round-44 wording "independent oracle" is withdrawn in favour of "second reader from another squashfs-tools release".

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kaidoku-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/2026-11-05-r92-stop-boundary-repair.md:47:2. **A pinned-image table passes the frozen C1 label.** A repair that writes the five pinned packed images and refuses otherwise meets `repaired 10 of 10` (measured by the critic, `c1-table-repair-5.json`, met True; and by MutCounter N1i). /home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kaidoku-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/2026-11-05-r92-stop-boundary-repair.md:52:7. **Packer dependence.** The result depends on UPX 5.2.0 (sha256 `cf2f6e7c6846a7c9a2224bc2bcc2dd79db6847d2cc73092d7356ce5c9aa8c969`). The oracle is UPX itself. [INFERENCE] A packer whose `upx -t` verdict disagreed with its own decoder would change the outcome.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kaidoku-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/2026-11-05-r93-fresh-pack-oracle.md:31:5. **A roundtrip oracle.** A cell classified `repaired` must additionally satisfy `upx -d` on the repair's output: the decompressed bytes' sha256 must equal the sha256 of the *mutated source*. A repair that writes something that decompresses to the wrong bytes is reclassified `roundtrip-fail` and is not counted as repaired. The round-92 label has no such clause — it can only compare bytes, so a repair that "repairs" to a self-consistent but wrong image was invisible to it.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kaidoku-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/2026-11-05-r93-fresh-pack-oracle.md:67:[INFERENCE] The floor is also the reason the arm needs the roundtrip oracle and not only a fresh carrier: a walk-based forger that computes a *plausible but wrong* value would pass a bare fresh-carrier check if it merely produced some output, whereas the roundtrip clause demands the original bytes back.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kaidoku-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/2026-11-05-r93-fresh-pack-oracle.md:133:1. **The v3 counter cannot write its own receipt when a fresh near cell is repaired.** `score_fresh_cell` keeps the repair's raw output bytes in `row["output"]`, and `json.dump` cannot serialize `bytes`. The run therefore **crashes with `TypeError: Object of type bytes is not JSON serializable` when serializing dict item 'output'`**, after the score is computed but before the receipt is complete; the process exits 1 and the `--json-out` file is left as a truncated, unparseable JSON prefix. Measured: the honest repair under v3 with `--json-out` exits 1 with that traceback and a 706-byte unparseable file, while the identical run **without** `--json-out` prints `repaired 12 of 12; …; fresh-roundtrip 2 of 2 met True exit 0` and exits 0. The same crash hits F3 (`identity_noop.py`) and the oracle-swap probe, for the same reason (both produce output bytes). It does **not** hit F1 or F2, whose fresh cells are refusals with no output file — which is exactly why the committed probe receipt `.ufo/waves/round-93/pre/probe-v3-F1-unscored.json` parses cleanly while the positive control's does not. **The frozen counter's sha256 is `60026511…`, unchanged in this round; this entry does not edit it.** The after-receipt at the measured revision must therefore be taken without `--json-out` (stdout only), or the receipt must be recorded by a caller that writes the label and the counts itself — the L1's decision, not this seat's.

The stored `sz_cpr` was 575 and the PackHeader offset 34400, and `last.block + 12 + stored = 30428 ≠ 34400`, so the chain does not even land on the PackHeader. **Observation:** the pristine value is not recoverable from the walk's landing point, which is why the packer's own verdict is the oracle. This is the geometric counterpart of QUALITY row 179 and of the round-91 finding. Probes retained at `.ufo/waves/round-93/w1/ScribeKnowledge/probes/p2-geometric.py` and `…/p3-geomwalk.py`.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kengaku-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/tb2-presence-only-triage.md:7:S194 defines an oracle as a procedure distinguishing correct from incorrect behaviour. An existence-only assertion distinguishes missing from present artefacts; it does not establish content correctness. S195 separates observable outcomes from admissible processes. S196 separates passive false-positive verification from active reward hacking. Accordingly, a P1 label here is not a claim that an entire task can be passed without solving it, nor evidence that any model exploited it.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:46:**Why this is not filler.** Divination is the load-bearing institution in fourteen civilization bases of this corpus, and it is the one myth-adjacent system that *produces institutions, offices, archives, registers and legal procedure* rather than only beliefs. A worldbuilder who has a pantheon and a cosmography but no reading technology has a world where nobody can ask the gods anything — which is historically the rare case. The entry also fills the lane's own namesake gap: 言霊 *kotodama* is a claim about the power of words, and the chance-utterance omen (Greek *klēdōn*), the oracle's verse, the chanted verse corpus of Ifa and the norito are exactly the places where that claim is operationalized (§7).
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:54:1. **Question** — what may be asked, in what form. Roman *auspicia* were yes/no per action ("should this assembly proceed?"), not general forecasts; Azande poison-oracle questions were propositions the oracle was asked to kill or spare the fowl over (Evans-Pritchard 1937); Ifa questions are open and the answer is a *verse* that must be applied (Bascom 1969).

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:97:1. **Closure.** The sign space must be small enough that disputants can enumerate it. This is the same closure principle the symbol register files use (`symbolism-synthesis-method.md` §1, §3: a grammar, not an encyclopedia) applied to procedure. Consequence for fiction: an oracle with an unbounded sign space cannot be *disputed*, so it can only be obeyed or destroyed — it is a plot device, not an institution.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:99:3. **The double-check.** The Azande second-oracle procedure re-asks the same proposition in a differently administered run; a contradiction is itself a sign (the oracle is refusing, or a taboo is active) — not evidence the method fails (Evans-Pritchard 1937). This is the single most useful institutional detail in the whole literature: it converts a failed reading into *more* procedure.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:101:5. **Cost.** A consultation costs something (a fee, a fowl, a tide-wait, a pilgrimage), so answers carry stakes and clients do not consult idly. Cost is also the mechanism that makes oracle-shopping visible: multiple oracles in one city produce competing institutions and a market in verdicts.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:125:| Arbiter | Azande poison oracle; oath-and-ordeal procedures | oracle operator + witnesses + chief's court | divination fused to law; the verdict is evidence |
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:135:- **The byproduct rule.** Every omen institution produces a second, unintended product: astronomy from astral omens, historiography from prodigy lists, medicine from incubation case archives, law from oracle arbitration, and script literacy from the Shang bone archive (Keightley 1978). When designing, ask what the archive *accidentally* becomes — that is usually the most interesting thing in the world.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:141:- **The chance utterance (*klēdōn*).** A word overheard is taken as the answer — the cheapest oracle in the world and the one that needs no institution. *Odyssey* 17 (a sneeze read as a sign) and 20 (the mill-woman's prayer-words); Xenophon's sneeze at *Anabasis* 3.2. Design lever: in a culture with word-omens, *ambient speech becomes evidence* — and the fiction must then police who may speak near a rite (couples `../linguistics/politeness-taboo-euphemism.md`).
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:143:- **The oracle's own register.** Delphic responses in verse, later in prose — Plutarch, himself a Delphic priest, treats the decline of verse as a symptom (*De Pythiae oraculis*). Ifa verses are chanted in an archaic Yoruba register and constitute a memorized literary corpus (Bascom 1969; Abimbola 1976). Norito are fixed liturgical formulas (*Engishiki*, 927 CE; trans. Philippi 1959). Craft rule: **an oracle speaks in a marked register**, and the register's archaism is datable evidence — a fictional oracle whose speech drifts toward the vernacular has a decline story built in.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:187:- **The Azande "closed system."** Evans-Pritchard's account (1937) is the classic, but the reading that the Azande *cannot* see contradictions is a construction of the later rationality debate (Winch 1964; Wilson ed. 1970) as much as of the ethnography. The safe craft lesson is procedural (the second-oracle rule), not psychological.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:202:5. **Give the system one anti-error rule** (counter-cast, paired charges, second oracle) and one procedural escape (taboo, impurity, bad day). Without both, the first failed prophecy ends the institution.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/divination-omen-systems.md:207:10. **Failure modes to avoid**: an oracle with no closed code (undisputable, therefore unplayable); a system with no procedural escape (dies at first failure); a diviner who is never wrong and never doubted (not an institution, a narrator); sign spaces copied wholesale from one real culture onto a fictional one without the frame-of-reference and color-capacity checks (`symbolism-synthesis-method.md` §4.5); ritual detail lifted from living traditions instead of invented; and *retrospective prophecy* used to make a faction look right — the readers of a world should be as accountable as its generals.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/sacred-kingship-mythic-charter.md:119:2. **The finding** — how the candidate is identified: descent audit, omen, oracle, election, the predecessor's designation, capture. This beat is where the polity's second institution (priests, diviners, nobles, army) shows its hand.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/myth-symbol/sacred-kingship-mythic-charter.md:160:- **The re-legitimation toolkit.** When a charter fails (a usurper, a lost war, a broken taboo), polities repair it with a small, recurring set of moves: **rewrite the genealogy** (annex a god, adopt a lineage); **find an object** (a relic, a regalia piece, a tomb — the found title-deed channel of §2.8); **obtain an oracle** (the divination file's whole apparatus, `divination-omen-systems.md`, exists to ratify or refuse); **re-stage the installation** (a second coronation, which is why §2.6's rite is repeatable); **change the name** (regnal and era names, §8.7); and **transfer the mandate** (the *translatio imperii* move, `era-periodization-models.md:75`). Design use: hand the usurper two of these moves and the story of a successful usurpation writes itself.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/verification/internal-consistency-audit.md:31:- **Honkasalo, Sami (2011).** “A Cognitive Approach to Character Formation in the Oracle Bone Script.” *Tokyo University Linguistic Papers (TULIP)* 31: 67–77 ([PDF read 2026-10-17](https://repository.dl.itc.u-tokyo.ac.jp/record/27548/files/ggr031005.pdf)). Basis: the rebus principle borrows an existing symbol “to write a homophonous or near homophonous word,” and its examples rest on pronunciation reconstructed for that stage (the preposition ‘from’ sharing the word for ‘nose’ in Shang Chinese); modern homophony cannot justify a rebus in an earlier stage.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kumitate-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/mount-order-and-bind-scope.md:7:Bubblewrap applies filesystem operations in argument order (S095, bwrap.xml:253-255: “These are applied in the order they are given as arguments.”). A later mount operation at the same destination covers what was mounted there earlier. `--bind SRC DEST` exposes the host path at DEST; `--tmpfs DEST` mounts a new tmpfs there (S095, bwrap.xml:295-312 and :410-416). Thus `--tmpfs /tmp --bind /tmp /tmp` replaces the runner's private tmpfs with the host `/tmp` read-write.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kumitate-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/mount-order-and-bind-scope.md:9:`cap-run` mounts fresh `/tmp` and `/run` tmpfs roots before appending bind requests and its implicit cwd bind. Refuse a bind that targets the whole `/tmp` root and refuse a cwd that resolves to a sandbox-managed mount root/tree; do not refuse every path beneath `/tmp`, because a caller may legitimately bind a specific fixture directory there. This rule governs cap-run's own CLI; direct bwrap callers remain outside that parser.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/revenger-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/round-63-zip-central-directory-evidence.md:10:# ZIP local header, central directory and end-of-central-directory records of one authored archive, compared with an authored oracle and a saved inventory record — C06
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/revenger-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/round-63-zip-central-directory-evidence.md:12:Scope. One authored archive, `corpus/fixtures/archive-r2/fixture.zip`, and two documents that describe it: the authored oracle `corpus/fixtures/archive-r2/truth.json` and the saved inspection record `corpus/live/runs/20261012T164246Z/artifact-inspection.json`. The analysis is **manual**: a Python standard-library script that reads the archive's bytes with `struct` and inflates the stored DEFLATE payload with `zlib`. Nothing is extracted to disk, no member is executed, no producer is called, no network is used, and no verifier source is read. The comparison is **one fixture's central-directory view**: the member names, local-header offsets, compressed and uncompressed sizes and CRC-32 values the central directory states, against an independent oracle, against the literal fields of one saved record. It is not a ZIP parser, not an archive inventory tool, and not a statement about any provider's accuracy.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/revenger-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/round-64-firmware-region-byte-evidence.md:10:Scope. One authored 2048-byte file, `corpus/fixtures/firmware-r63/firmware-r63.bin`, and the documents that describe it: the authored oracle `corpus/fixtures/firmware-r63/truth.json`, the generator that wrote both, and the saved round-63 record `corpus/live/runs/round-63/inspection.json`, whose own envelope attributes it to a Binwalk 3.1.0 `inspect_firmware_regions` run, with its receipt and ledger. The analysis is **manual and standard-library only**: a Python script reads the file's bytes, inflates the zlib streams in memory, and hashes the inflated bytes. Nothing is extracted to disk, executed, emulated or mounted; no firmware tool or provider is invoked; no verifier source is read. The comparison is **one fixture's region view**: every all-zero padding span and both compressed members with their decompressed sizes and digests, checked field-by-field against an authored oracle and against the two regions the saved Binwalk run reported. It is not a vendor or device image, not evidence about any real firmware, not an extraction or execution claim, not a provider-completeness claim and not an authenticity claim. "Firmware" here is the C07 coverage label for the route that produced the record, not a format or family identification.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/revenger-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/round-64-firmware-region-byte-evidence.md:163:What the agreement does **not** establish (Q09). A SHA-256 match proves only that the bytes hashed are the bytes named: not who authored them, not that the oracle is canonical or unique, not that Binwalk's signature inference is correct, and not that the fixture resembles any real firmware. The record's own `raw_result` shows it ran on a private workspace copy (`/tmp/rea-firmware-7rPoLl/firmware.bin`); the link to the fixture is the record's own subject digest and `input_size`, plus S036's statement that region offsets refer to bytes in the original firmware. The oracle's `padding_byte_hex`/`fill_hex` values and member labels are recorded metadata compared for correspondence, not independently authenticated facts.

One authored APK, `corpus/fixtures/apk-r65/fixture.apk`, and the authored oracle that declares it, `corpus/fixtures/apk-r65/truth.json`. /home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tameshi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/runner-tally-audit.md:1:source: S097 — GNU Bash Reference Manual, Bash 5.3 source `doc/bashref.texi`: §Exit Status (the 0–255 status range the oracle sweeps) and §Conditional Constructs `case` (the first matching pattern wins, so an arm inserted before `*)` reclassifies a status); S099 — PIT home page (a test suite is judged by the seeded faults it kills); S094 — Dwork et al., arXiv:1411.2664v3 §1 (a holdout the candidate never sees; re-opened round 32, analogy only)
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tameshi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/runner-tally-audit.md:12:2. **Runtime tally against an oracle.** The tally block is cut from the runner and run in bash with the nine statuses bound by name. It is run for every exit status 0–255 at the first leg, statuses 1 2 3 4 126 127 255 at every leg, multi-status vectors, and the loop with one operand duplicated or removed at each position. The exact first line and the exit code are both compared with an oracle written from the runner's printed contract: any 1 → FAIL exit 1; else any non-zero → SETUP exit 2; else a count other than `nlegs` → SETUP case-tally mismatch; else PASS. Bash exit statuses fall between 0 and 255 (S097), so the sweep is exhaustive for one leg. `case` runs the command list of the first pattern that matches (S097), so an arm inserted before `*)` reclassifies exactly the statuses it names. A sweep that stops at 3 misses an arm for 4 (MutateHead T08) or for any other value.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tameshi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/runner-tally-audit.md:24:- The oracle restates the runner's own printed contract. If that contract is wrong, the audit agrees with it.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tameshi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/r6-flaky-failures-cluster.md:34:F8 (`corpus/README.md` F8 row: oracle anchoring as a survivor class; next move "one survivor classified against an independently anchored expectation") is the question this section reads the abstract against; the abstract says nothing about test oracles or mutation survivors. The located premise is that the members of a cluster have a shared cause: "co-occurring failures that share the same root causes" (8 tokens). Inference: if the members of a cluster share a root cause, an expectation anchored on one member's observed outcome is not evidence about the other members' outcomes, so two survivors from one cluster would supply one anchor, not two, and a per-survivor verdict table that does not record cluster membership would overstate the number of independent anchors it holds.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/limine-pin-writers-r88.md:33:| PW6 | [src] limine-snapper-sync builds a snapshot entry from the main entry's `path:` line: it cuts the first `#hex` run off that line (pattern `#[a-fA-F0-9]*`) and appends that same text to the history-file path it writes; the history file itself is a plain byte copy of the live ESP file, and the snapshot path line is emitted from that stored text. It never recomputes the BLAKE2b, and cannot: its hash set is sha1, sha256, blake3 and xxhash, no BLAKE2b. Its "first `#`, any length" cut differs from Limine's own "last `#`, exactly 128 hex" (r87 LP3); on this host every path carries one `#`, so the two agree. | S359 KernelReader.java 154-157, 175-179, 196-213; Limine.java 28; Hash.java 9-12; SnapshotManager.java 212-243; ImageDetail.java 47-50 | "String snapshotPathValue = Utility.buildPath(config.machineID(), Config.HISTORY_FOLDER, fileHashName + blake2sum);" | [obs] existing history pins (lines 50, 56, 64) date from 2026-10-20, before the incident; r87 read them OK |
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/limine-pin-writers-r88.md:34:| PW7 | [src] The history file is named after the current file's content hash (`HASH_FUNCTION`, default SHA-256), computed fresh at sync time. Its integrity check compares that name suffix with the content, never the `#` pin. One snapshot path line therefore carries two different hashes: a 64-hex SHA-256 inside the file name (fresh, of the bytes just copied) and a 128-hex BLAKE2b after `#` (copied text, never recomputed), which Limine reads as the pin. | S359 KernelReader.java 196-209; Hash.java 60-66; Config.java 46; SnapshotManager.java 212-243 | "return !hashedFilePath.endsWith(hashSum);  // true = corrupted" | [obs] history names end `_sha256_<64 hex>`; line 50 reads `…omarchy_linux.efi_sha256_2e9c9bdc…#822299bb…` |

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/limine-uri-failure-r89.md:30:| UF2 | [src] After one leading `$` is dropped, the resource is compared with `strcmp` (case-sensitive) against `hdd`, `odd`, `boot`, `guid`, `uuid` (same dispatcher as `guid`), `fslabel`, `tftp`; any other name is "not valid" and goes to `URI_ERROR`, i.e. a panic for a required key. | S351 uri.c 344-366 | "URI_ERROR(\"Resource `%s` not valid.\", resource);" (line 364) | [obs] all six URIs in `/boot/limine.conf` are `boot():/…` (r88 host receipt pin lines 30, 38, 50, 56, 64, 72) |
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/limine-uri-failure-r89.md:31:| UF3 | [src] `boot()` with an empty root opens the partition Limine booted from; `boot(N)` opens partition N (0-256, parsed with `strtoui`) on the SAME drive as the boot volume; N > 256 is a `URI_ERROR`; on a PXE boot `boot()` and `boot(N)` are redirected to `tftp` (lines 221-222, before the partition check). So `boot(N)` is resolvable by Limine — the gate's `LP-UNRESOLVED` for it is a gate limit (it hashes only `--esp`), not a Limine verdict. | S351 uri.c 220-243 | "struct volume *volume = volume_get_by_coord(boot_volume->is_optical, boot_volume->index, partition);" (lines 237-238) | [obs] no `boot(N)` on this host (UF2), so the limit does not fire here |
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/limine-uri-failure-r89.md:33:| UF5 | [src] An `efi` entry (`protocol: efi`, `chainload()`) reads `PATH`, then `IMAGE_PATH`; if `uri_open` returns NULL — file absent, volume not found, or a value `uri_resolve` cannot split (no `(` or no `):/`, which returns false without a message, lines 46-63) — Limine panics with "Failed to open image". So `LP-MISSING`, an unresolvable volume, and a URI-less `path:` value are all a panic of that entry at boot, not an ignored line. | S361 chainload.c 414-435; S351 uri.c 46-63 | "panic(true, \"efi: Failed to open image with path `%s`. Is the path correct?\", image_path);" (line 435) | [obs] all six entries are `protocol: efi` (host receipt lines 29, 37, 49, 55, 63, 71) |
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/limine-uri-failure-r89.md:34:| UF6 | [src] Around that `uri_open`, `chainload()` sets `secure_boot_active = false` and restores it after; inside `uri_open` that flag gates only the "Secure Boot is active and URI has no associated hash" error. So an `efi` entry's `path:` may be unhashed under Secure Boot (the firmware's LoadImage verifies the image instead), while a pin that IS present is still compared (the mismatch path does not read the flag). The code comment says Limine "does not need to enforce its own hash check here"; the code only waives the missing-hash rule. Under Secure Boot, menu.c also forces `hash_mismatch_panic` on (menu.c 1714-1717), so a present-pin mismatch there is a `URI_ERROR` panic, not the Y-prompt. | S361 chainload.c 423-437; S351 uri.c 372-376, 442, 470, 664; S353 menu.c 1714-1717 | "// The firmware's LoadImage will verify the Secure Boot signature of the chainloaded EFI application, so Limine does not need" (lines 423-424) | [obs] SecureBoot efivar data byte `00` (off) |
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/limine-uri-failure-r89.md:35:| UF7 | [src] Every panic above is `panic(true, …)`: it prints `PANIC`, then "Press a key to return to menu", and the menu comes back, so another entry (a snapshot entry, the fallback) can be chosen — on UEFI only while the firmware's boot services are still up (`efi_boot_services_exited == false`, lines 71-77), which holds for a path that fails to open. Under measured boot the menu return is refused and the machine prints "System halted." (UEFI; a BIOS build prints a CTRL+ALT+DEL line instead). Measured boot is on only when the config says exactly `measured_boot: yes` or Secure Boot is active, and is forced off when the firmware exposes no TPM/CC measurement interface. Limine's "Secure Boot active" is itself narrower than the firmware flag: `init_config` clears it when no config checksum is enrolled (config.c 488-492). Narrowing from wave seat ScoutSources (SR11, SR18, SR21). | S362 panic.s2.c 18-29, 70-100; S353 menu.c 1719-1730; S350 CONFIG.md 157-160; S352 config.c 488-492 | "print(\"Press a key to return to %s.\", booting_from_editor ? \"editor\" : \"menu\");" (panic.s2.c line 78) | [obs] no `measured_boot` key in `/boot/limine.conf`; Secure Boot off; `/sys/class/tpm/` empty |

The stub runs as an image Limine itself started (`LoadImage`/`StartImage`, chainload.c 505-541) while boot services are still up, so it asked the same firmware session. One gap keeps this an inference: a TPM with the flag set whose `GetActivePcrBanks` fails would also read 0. With 0 (or with no `measured_boot` key and Secure Boot off, as here) measured boot is off and every `panic` consequence returns to the menu: the live gate reads `on_panic: menu` without the flag. | TV1-TV3, TV9; S361 chainload.c 505-541; S353 menu.c 1719-1728; S362 | — | live gate at `ce89df93`: `on_panic menu`, `measured_boot None` (`.ufo/waves/round-90/after/live-receipt.txt`) |

A GPT partition's number is its entry slot plus 1 (`partition + 1`), and empty slots are skipped without renumbering, so N is a slot number with gaps, not a count of partitions (wave seat ScoutVolume's finding 2). [obs] Limine's own `LoaderDevicePartUUID` names `b80445be-…`, the ESP, which Linux numbers partition 1 of `nvme0n1` (Linux numbers GPT slots the same way). [inf] So `boot()` and `boot(1)` name the same volume here. `hdd(d:p)` needs Limine's firmware drive index, which Linux does not show; no `hdd()` URI is mapped. | S351 uri.c 220-243; S366 part.s2.c 575; S373 disk.s2.c 1004-1016; S368 bli.c 110-124; S371 | "ret->partition   = partition + 1;" (part.s2.c line 575) | `LoaderDevicePartUUID` = `b80445be-18ee-45e6-9a30-14339ea785a7`; ESP PARTN 1 |
